Risk-Adjusted Harm Scoring for Automated Red Teaming for LLMs in Financial Services
Authors: Fabrizio Dimino, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
First: 2026-03-11T14:14:13+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T14:24:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Existing LLM safety evaluations rely on binary attack-success rates and domain-agnostic taxonomies, leaving regulated Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) deployments exposed to failures elicited through legally or professionally plausible framing. We introduce RAHS (Risk-Adjusted Harm Score), a risk-sensitive metric jointly capturing disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement, and FinRedTeamBench, a 989-prompt benchmark spanning seven BFSI risk areas and 34 sub-categories mapped to regulatory frameworks. Evaluation uses an ensemble of three heterogeneous LLM judges, validated against human experts, and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline. On nine open-weight models, RAHS preserves separation under near-ceiling ASR, ranking is stable under hyperparameter sweeps, and multi-turn pressure drives not only more jailbreaks but more operationally severe disclosures, exposing failure modes that single-turn, domain-agnostic evaluations cannot reveal.
中文标题/摘要
标题：金融服务中针对大型语言模型的自动红队风险调整危害评分
现有的大型语言模型安全评估依赖于二元攻击成功率和领域无关的分类法，使受监管的银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）部署面临通过合法或专业上合理的框架引发的失败。我们引入了RAHS（风险调整危害评分），这是一种风险敏感的指标，联合捕捉披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者间一致性，以及FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖七个BFSI风险领域和34个子类别的989个提示基准，映射到监管框架。评估使用三位异构大型语言模型评审者的集成，经过与人类专家的验证，以及自适应多轮红队流程。在九个开放权重模型上，RAHS在接近上限的攻击成功率下保持分离，排名在超参数搜索中稳定，多轮压力不仅驱动更多越狱行为，还导致更具操作严重性的披露，暴露出单轮、领域无关评估无法揭示的失败模式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing safety evaluations for large language models (LLMs) in the Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) sectors, which often rely on binary attack-success rates and lack specificity to the domain, potentially leaving these systems vulnerable. Previous methods have not adequately captured the nuances of risk in this context, prompting the development of the Risk-Adjusted Harm Score (RAHS), which integrates disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement into a comprehensive metric. The paper contributes a novel evaluation framework, FinRedTeamBench, which includes a benchmark of 989 prompts across various BFSI risk areas, and employs an ensemble of three diverse LLM judges validated against human experts. The methodology demonstrates that RAHS maintains effective differentiation among models even at high attack success rates, revealing critical vulnerabilities that traditional single-turn evaluations miss, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety in regulated environments.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLMs）在银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）领域安全评估的局限性，这些评估通常依赖于二元攻击成功率和通用分类法，使这些系统容易受到微妙失败的影响。所提出的方法风险调整伤害评分（RAHS）通过引入一种风险敏感的指标来评估披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者一致性，与过去的方法不同，从而提供了更全面的评估，符合监管要求。本文的贡献包括开发了FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖七个BFSI风险领域的989个提示的基准，以及使用经过人类专家验证的三种异构LLM评审者的集成方法。该方法涉及自适应多轮红队测试流程，证明RAHS在九个开放权重模型中保持了性能稳定性，并揭示了关键的失败模式，超越了传统的单轮评估，支持了在受监管环境中需要更强大安全评估的必要性。
Do We Still Need Humans in the Loop? Human vs. LLM Annotation in Active Learning for TikTok Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Ahmad Dawar Hakimi, Lea Hirlimann, Isabelle Augenstein, Hinrich Schütze
First: 2026-04-15T14:10:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T11:56:04+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Annotating data remains a costly bottleneck for supervised NLP. Active learning (AL) reduces the number of human labels needed by selecting only the most informative instances, while instruction-tuned LLMs attack the same bottleneck from the other side, making labels cheap enough to annotate entire corpora. This raises two questions: can LLM labels replace human labels within the AL loop, and does AL remain necessary when entire corpora can be cheaply labeled? We investigate both by training supervised hate speech classifiers on a new dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments, comparing human and LLM annotation under matched conditions. LLM annotation at scale outperforms human-supervised classifiers at roughly one-tenth the cost, for both a closed-source (GPT-5.2) and an open-weight (Qwen3.5-122B-A10B) LLM, and the advantage is robust under soft-label evaluation. It hinges on the annotation interface: only a two-question decomposition mirroring the human annotation task unlocks it. AL provides no reliable advantage over random sampling in our prefiltered pool. Error structure depends on the LLM: only GPT-5.2 matches the human FP/FN balance, while other variants over-flag border-control and economic-competition discourse. Humans remain essential as evaluators; for training labels, the question shifts to which LLM, which interface, and what shape of pool.
中文标题/摘要
标题：我们还需要人类参与吗？在TikTok仇恨言论检测中人类与LLM注释的主动学习比较
数据注释仍然是监督NLP的一个成本瓶颈。主动学习（AL）通过仅选择最具信息量的实例来减少所需的人类标签数量，而经过指令调优的LLM则从另一个角度解决同一瓶颈，使标签变得足够便宜以注释整个语料库。这引发了两个问题：LLM标签能否替代AL循环中的人类标签，以及当整个语料库可以被廉价标注时，AL是否仍然必要？我们通过在一个包含278K条德国政治TikTok评论的新数据集上训练监督仇恨言论分类器来研究这两个问题，比较在匹配条件下的人类和LLM注释。大规模LLM注释的表现优于人类监督的分类器，成本约为十分之一，适用于封闭源（GPT-5.2）和开放权重（Qwen3.5-122B-A10B）LLM，并且在软标签评估下优势稳健。这依赖于注释接口：只有与人类注释任务相对应的两个问题的分解才能解锁它。在我们预过滤的池中，AL对随机抽样没有可靠的优势。错误结构依赖于LLM：只有GPT-5.2匹配人类的FP/FN平衡，而其他变体则过度标记边境控制和经济竞争话语。人类作为评估者仍然至关重要；对于训练标签，问题转向哪个LLM、哪个接口以及什么形状的池。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the challenge of data annotation in supervised natural language processing (NLP), particularly in the context of hate speech detection on TikTok, where traditional methods are costly and time-consuming. Previous approaches relied heavily on human annotation, which is limited by scalability and expense, while recent advancements in instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) offer a potential alternative by providing cheaper labels. The proposed study investigates whether LLM-generated labels can effectively replace human annotations in an active learning (AL) framework, using a dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments. The findings reveal that LLM annotation significantly outperforms human annotation at a fraction of the cost, although the effectiveness is contingent on the annotation interface design. The research concludes that while LLMs can provide valuable training labels, human evaluators remain crucial for assessing model performance, suggesting a nuanced role for both humans and LLMs in the annotation process.
本研究解决了监督自然语言处理（NLP）中数据标注成本高昂的问题，特别是在TikTok仇恨言论检测的背景下。以往的方法主要依赖人工标注，这既昂贵又耗时，而最近的指令调优大型语言模型（LLMs）使得以更低成本生成标签成为可能。本研究旨在评估LLM生成的标签是否可以有效替代人工标签在主动学习（AL）过程中的作用，以及当整个数据集可以低成本标注时，AL是否仍然必要。研究方法包括在一个包含278,000条德国政治TikTok评论的数据集上训练仇恨言论分类器，比较使用人工与LLM标注的分类器性能。研究结果表明，LLM标注在成本仅为人工标注十分之一的情况下显著优于人工标注，其有效性依赖于所使用的标注接口。然而，AL未能提供比随机抽样更可靠的优势，尽管LLM可以高效生成标签，但人工仍然在评估过程中至关重要，这表明LLM和人工标注者在标注过程中的角色应更为细致。
Turning the Spell Around: Lightweight Alignment Amplification via Rank-One Safety Injection
Authors: Harethah Abu Shairah, Hasan Abed Al Kader Hammoud, George Turkiyyah, Bernard Ghanem
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2025-08-28T13:22:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T07:43:32+00:00
Comments: EMNLP 2026, Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs) often involves mediating internal representations to refuse harmful requests. Recent research has demonstrated that these safety mechanisms can be bypassed by ablating or removing specific representational directions within the model. In this paper, we propose the opposite approach: Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), a white-box method that amplifies a model's safety alignment by permanently steering its activations toward the refusal-mediating subspace. ROSI operates as a simple, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification applied to all residual stream write matrices. The required safety direction can be computed from a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs. We show that ROSI consistently increases safety refusal rates - as evaluated by Llama Guard 3 - while preserving the utility of the model on standard benchmarks such as MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. Furthermore, we show that ROSI can also re-align 'uncensored' models by amplifying their own latent safety directions, demonstrating its utility as an effective last-mile safety procedure. Our results suggest that targeted, interpretable weight steering is a cheap and potent mechanism to improve LLM safety, complementing more resource-intensive fine-tuning paradigms.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扭转咒语：通过秩一安全注入实现轻量级对齐放大
大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全对齐通常涉及调解内部表示以拒绝有害请求。最近的研究表明，这些安全机制可以通过消除或移除模型内特定的表示方向来绕过。在本文中，我们提出了相反的方法：秩一安全注入（ROSI），这是一种白盒方法，通过永久性地将模型的激活引导到拒绝调解子空间来放大模型的安全对齐。ROSI作为一种简单的、无需微调的秩一权重修改，应用于所有残差流写矩阵。所需的安全方向可以从一小组有害和无害的指令对中计算得出。我们展示了ROSI在Llama Guard 3的评估中持续提高安全拒绝率，同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持模型的效用。此外，我们还展示了ROSI可以通过放大自身潜在安全方向来重新对齐“未审查”模型，证明其作为有效的最后一公里安全程序的实用性。我们的结果表明，针对性的、可解释的权重引导是一种廉价而有效的机制，可以提高LLM的安全性，补充更资源密集的微调范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), where existing methods often fail due to the ability of harmful requests to bypass safety mechanisms by altering internal representations. The proposed approach, Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), differs from past methods by amplifying safety alignment rather than attempting to mediate harmful requests, utilizing a straightforward rank-one weight modification that steers model activations toward a refusal-mediating subspace without requiring fine-tuning. This method is well-motivated as it leverages a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs to compute the necessary safety direction. The contribution of the paper lies in demonstrating that ROSI significantly increases safety refusal rates while maintaining model performance on standard benchmarks like MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc, thus supporting its goal of enhancing LLM safety through a cost-effective mechanism. The results indicate that ROSI can also realign uncensored models by amplifying their latent safety directions, showcasing its effectiveness as a last-mile safety procedure.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全对齐的挑战，现有方法常常因有害请求能够通过改变特定内部表示而绕过安全机制而失效。所提出的方法，称为秩一安全注入（ROSI），与过去的方法不同，它通过增强安全对齐而不是试图调解有害请求，从而提供了更强大的解决方案。ROSI是一种轻量级的、无需微调的技术，通过修改残差流写入矩阵中的秩一权重，利用一小组有害和无害指令对来计算所需的安全方向。论文表明，ROSI显著提高了安全拒绝率（通过Llama Guard 3评估），同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持了模型性能，表明该方法有效增强了LLM的安全性而不损害其效用。
TACS: Trajectory-Aware Candidate Selection for LLM Jailbreak Suffix Optimization
Authors: Shiliang Xiao
First: 2026-08-30T05:18:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:18:28+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods typically update the suffix by retaining the candidate with the lowest current loss. We show that this seemingly natural design is fundamentally myopic: candidates that look better under the current-step proxy often fail to produce better jailbreak outcomes later in the search, revealing a form of selection-stage reward hacking. This suggests that candidate selection, rather than candidate generation alone, is a hidden bottleneck in suffix optimization. To address this issue, we propose \OURS{}, a trajectory-aware candidate selection framework for jailbreak suffix optimization. Instead of selecting candidates solely by their immediate loss, \OURS{} augments per-step evaluation with a trajectory-aware proxy and stabilizes selection with reference-policy regularization and a discriminator-estimated chi-squared correction, encouraging choices that remain effective beyond the current step. Experiments on HarmBench show that \OURS{} consistently outperforms strong baselines under the same search budget, substantially improving attack success rates while exhibiting more stable optimization behavior throughout the search. Our findings highlight that mitigating selection-stage reward hacking caused by myopic candidate selection is critical for improving jailbreak suffix optimization.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TACS：基于轨迹的LLM越狱后缀优化候选选择
基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法通常通过保留当前损失最低的候选来更新后缀。我们表明，这种看似自然的设计在根本上是短视的：在当前步骤代理下看起来更好的候选往往在搜索的后期未能产生更好的越狱结果，揭示了一种选择阶段奖励黑客行为。这表明候选选择，而不仅仅是候选生成，是后缀优化中的一个隐藏瓶颈。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了\OURS{}，一个用于越狱后缀优化的基于轨迹的候选选择框架。\OURS{}并不是仅通过候选的即时损失来选择，而是通过轨迹感知代理增强每一步的评估，并通过参考策略正则化和判别器估计的卡方修正来稳定选择，鼓励在当前步骤之外仍然有效的选择。在HarmBench上的实验表明，\OURS{}在相同搜索预算下始终优于强基线，显著提高了攻击成功率，同时在整个搜索过程中表现出更稳定的优化行为。我们的研究结果强调，减轻由短视候选选择引起的选择阶段奖励黑客行为对于改善越狱后缀优化至关重要。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing gradient-based jailbreak suffix optimization methods, which typically select candidates based solely on the lowest current loss, leading to suboptimal long-term outcomes due to myopic decision-making. The proposed method, TACS, introduces a trajectory-aware candidate selection framework that enhances candidate evaluation by incorporating a trajectory-aware proxy and stabilizing selection through reference-policy regularization and a discriminator-estimated chi-squared correction. This approach effectively mitigates the issue of selection-stage reward hacking, which has been identified as a bottleneck in suffix optimization. The methodology was tested on the HarmBench task, where TACS demonstrated significant improvements in attack success rates and more stable optimization behavior compared to strong baseline methods, thereby supporting the goal of enhancing jailbreak suffix optimization performance.
本研究解决了现有基于梯度的越狱后缀优化方法的局限性，这些方法通常仅根据候选者的即时损失进行选择，导致由于选择阶段奖励黑客行为而产生的长期结果不佳。所提出的方法TACS引入了一种轨迹感知的候选选择框架，通过结合轨迹感知代理和通过参考策略正则化及鉴别器估计的卡方修正来增强候选评估，从而稳定选择。该方法有效缓解了由短视候选选择引起的问题，在攻击成功率和优化稳定性方面显示出显著改善。在HarmBench上进行的实验确认，TACS在相同搜索预算内优于强基线方法，验证了其在增强越狱后缀优化方面的有效性。
Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:09:19+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Sci-FM Workshop @ COLM 2026 (non-archival). Workshop version with reviews: https://openreview.net/forum?id=mZh0MqpOOC
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，使用一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言中稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证），并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一转换是普遍最危险的（20个每个转换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark evaluations in assessing the safety of large language models (LLMs), which typically rely on a single canonical surface form for scoring. Previous methods have not adequately captured the variability in model behavior when the surface form changes, leading to potential underestimations of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts and scoring the responses with a consistent, human-anchored judge to ensure intent preservation. The methodology was tested on 370 seeds across five models and five surface forms, revealing that evaluating only the canonical prompt significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes, with the union of unsafe results across forms exceeding the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 percentage points. This study contributes to a better understanding of LLM safety by demonstrating the importance of surface form sensitivity and providing a dataset and tools for further research.
本研究探讨了使用单一标准提示评估大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性时的局限性，尤其是在没有明确金标准的高风险场景中。以往的方法依赖于固定的表面形式，这可能由于响应中的噪声和变异性而无法准确反映模型行为。所提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，以确保意图一致，同时变化表面形式，从而更全面地评估不同模型的安全合规性。研究方法包括对370个种子进行测试，使用五种表面形式和五个模型，结果显示仅依赖标准提示会低估潜在的安全风险。研究发现，不同形式的安全结果联合显著超过任何单一形式的结果，强调在安全评估中考虑多种表面形式的重要性。本文贡献了一个数据集和代码，以促进该领域的进一步研究。
NeuronFuzz: Safety Neuron Guided Fuzzing for LLM Safety Evaluation
Authors: Zhiyuan Xu, Muhammad Firhard Roslan, Joseph Gardiner, Sana Belguith, Lichao Wu
First: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation is critical for assessing whether aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) remain robust against jailbreak attacks. Existing automated testing methods, however, largely rely on response-level feedback: each candidate prompt typically requires generating a target-model response to evaluate its attack effectiveness. This process is expensive and, more importantly, provides only sparse guidance on strongly aligned models, where most candidates are rejected with the same failure outcome. This paper presents NeuronFuzz, a white-box fuzzing framework that exploits internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback for LLM safety evaluation. A SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score that serves as feedback for fuzzing and can be obtained during prefill, eliminating response generation from the fuzzing loop. To construct the SafetyOracle, NeuronFuzz uses template-invariant harmful and benign inputs and stability-aware selection to identify a compact set of safety neurons whose activations capture harmful-intent recognition. Moreover, since the safety alarm score is differentiable, NeuronFuzz uses its gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model to generate fluent, context-compatible mutations while preserving original harmful payload and avoiding additional optimization variables. We evaluate NeuronFuzz across 21 text and multimodal models. Across five white-box source models, it achieves a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. Its optimized templates further transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR (EASR) of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronFuzz：安全神经元引导的LLM安全评估模糊测试
安全评估对于评估对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）是否能够抵御越狱攻击至关重要。然而，现有的自动化测试方法主要依赖于响应级反馈：每个候选提示通常需要生成目标模型的响应以评估其攻击有效性。这个过程成本高昂，更重要的是，对于大多数候选者在强对齐模型中以相同的失败结果被拒绝，提供的指导非常稀疏。本文提出了NeuronFuzz，一个利用内部安全神经元作为LLM安全评估的连续执行反馈的白盒模糊测试框架。SafetyOracle将安全神经元的激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，作为模糊测试的反馈，并且可以在预填充期间获得，从而消除模糊测试循环中的响应生成。为了构建SafetyOracle，NeuronFuzz使用模板不变的有害和良性输入以及稳定性感知选择，识别出一组紧凑的安全神经元，其激活捕捉有害意图识别。此外，由于安全警报分数是可微分的，NeuronFuzz利用其梯度识别安全敏感的模板位置，并使用掩码语言模型生成流畅、上下文兼容的变异，同时保留原始有害负载并避免额外的优化变量。我们在21个文本和多模态模型上评估了NeuronFuzz。在五个白盒源模型中，它实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，超越基线最多48个百分点。其优化模板进一步零-shot转移到开放权重和六个专有目标模型，分别实现了69.6%/92.6%和44.1%/60.0%的平均ASR和前5集成ASR（EASR）。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for effective safety evaluation of aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks, highlighting the limitations of existing automated testing methods that rely on response-level feedback, which is costly and provides limited guidance. The proposed NeuronFuzz framework differs by utilizing internal safety neurons for continuous execution feedback, thus eliminating the need for response generation in the fuzzing process. This approach is well-motivated as it enhances the efficiency and effectiveness of safety evaluations. NeuronFuzz constructs a SafetyOracle that translates safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score, enabling the identification of safety-sensitive template positions and the generation of context-compatible mutations. The methodology is evaluated across 21 text and multimodal models, achieving a jailbreak discovery rate of 76-100% across five white-box source models, significantly outperforming existing baselines and demonstrating its capability to support robust safety evaluations.

'Ghaib in Translation' aka Unseen Harm: Measuring Cross-Script Safety Inconsistency with 'Missed-in-Urdu' Scores in LLM Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Fawzia Zehra, Kara-Isitt, Sonal Khosla, Stephen Swift
First: 2026-08-25T07:58:40+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T07:58:40+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Urdu, the world's tenth most spoken language with 246 million speakers, remains almost entirely absent from mainstream LLM safety evaluation and nine years of WOAH proceedings. To investigate whether this absence has measurable consequences for content moderation reliability, five large language models, GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Qwen-2.5, and Llama-3.1, were tested across six datasets spanning Nastaliq Urdu, Roman Urdu, English, and code-switched Urdu-English. Across the five Urdu-script datasets, label instability between original-script and English-translation classification ranged from 15.9% (Gemini 2.5 Flash) to 31.6% (Qwen-2.5), with a 'Missed-in-Urdu' rate, content flagged as harmful in English translation but passed as normal in the original script, ranging from 2.4% to 9.9% (median 4.3%). A complete enumeration of all 205 papers across nine ALW/WOAH editions via the ACL Anthology API confirms zero dedicated Urdu papers across the entire period. Results indicate that current LLMs provide uneven safety assurance across Urdu's script varieties, with smaller open-weight models showing substantially higher instability and missed-harm rates than frontier closed models.
中文标题/摘要
标题：'翻译中的隐秘'即未见的危害：使用'乌尔都语遗漏'分数测量跨脚本安全性不一致性在LLM仇恨言论检测中的应用
乌尔都语是全球第十大语言，拥有2.46亿说话者，但在主流LLM安全评估和九年的WOAH会议中几乎完全缺席。为了调查这种缺席是否对内容审核的可靠性产生可测量的影响，测试了五个大型语言模型：GPT-4o、Claude Sonnet 4.5、Gemini 2.5 Flash、Qwen-2.5和Llama-3.1，涵盖了六个数据集，包括Nastaliq乌尔都语、罗马乌尔都语、英语和代码切换的乌尔都语-英语。在五个乌尔都语脚本数据集中，原始脚本与英语翻译分类之间的标签不稳定性范围为15.9%（Gemini 2.5 Flash）到31.6%（Qwen-2.5），'乌尔都语遗漏'率，即在英语翻译中标记为有害但在原始脚本中通过的内容，范围为2.4%到9.9%（中位数4.3%）。通过ACL Anthology API对九个ALW/WOAH版本中205篇论文的完整枚举确认，在整个期间内没有专门的乌尔都语论文。结果表明，当前的LLM在乌尔都语的脚本变体中提供的不均匀安全保障，较小的开放权重模型显示出明显更高的不稳定性和遗漏危害率，超过了前沿的封闭模型。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of safety evaluation for Urdu, a widely spoken language, in large language models (LLMs), which has been overlooked in previous studies and proceedings. Past methods have failed to adequately assess content moderation reliability across different scripts, leading to inconsistencies in harm detection. This study proposes a novel approach by testing five LLMs on multiple datasets that include various Urdu scripts and English translations, revealing significant label instability and a notable 'Missed-in-Urdu' rate, indicating harmful content that goes undetected in the original script. The contribution of this paper lies in highlighting the disparities in safety assurance among different Urdu script varieties and demonstrating that smaller models exhibit higher rates of missed harm. The methodology involves a comprehensive evaluation of LLM performance across six datasets, ultimately showing that current models inadequately address safety in Urdu, thus supporting the need for improved evaluation frameworks for underrepresented languages.

ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
Venue: 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), Towson, MD, USA, 2026, pp. 414-417
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T05:47:14+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation. Published in the 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), pp. 414-417. Recipient of the Best Paper Award at IEEE/ACIS SERA 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数针对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估仅评估单个提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）上进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被视为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明在该评估环境中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力累积。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在训练分布外部署的微调攻击者的攻击者漂移作为失败模式，以及攻击者拒绝作为受害者抵抗测量中之前未报告的混淆因素。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of traditional adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, neglecting the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework introduces a continuous measurement of guardrail degradation using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model, which enhances reliability by eliminating safety refusals common in off-the-shelf models. This approach is well-motivated as it provides a more nuanced understanding of compliance through a structured scoring system. The study employs a controlled experiment with three advanced victim models and a triple-judge consensus architecture, revealing a 26.7% jailbreak rate across multiple adversarial rounds, indicating that vulnerabilities are more prevalent in initial interactions. The findings contribute to the understanding of judge reliability and the dynamics of adversarial interactions in LLMs, while also documenting various failure modes and limitations in existing evaluation methods.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）对抗性评估的局限性，这些评估通常通过单一提示和二元结果来评估安全性，未能捕捉到在持续对抗互动中安全属性的动态变化。所提出的方法ADVERSA引入了一种自动化红队框架，将护栏退化测量为连续的合规轨迹，而不是离散事件，利用经过微调的70B攻击者模型来提高对受害者响应评分的可靠性。本文通过提供结构化的评判可靠性方法和记录各种失败模式（包括评审者间一致性和攻击者漂移）为该领域做出了贡献。该方法涉及对三个先进受害者模型进行的受控实验，跨多个对抗回合揭示了26.7%的越狱率集中在早期互动中，从而支持了在持续压力下对LLM安全性进行持续评估的必要性。
NeuronGuard: Robust LLM Safety Alignment via Ablation-Aware Safety Signal Redistribution
Authors: Anjun Gao, Yueyang Quan, Yufei Xia, Zhuqing Liu, Minghong Fang
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00
Comments: To appear in EMNLP 2026 (Findings)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) remains brittle against a growing spectrum of attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks directly prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit a common weakness: safety-relevant information concentrates in a sparse neuron subset. We present NeuronGuard, a fine-tuning-stage defense that simultaneously hardens LLMs against both attack classes by redistributing safety signals across a broader set of neurons. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between the defense and task objectives. We provide a formal guarantee that NeuronGuard strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound, and experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronGuard：通过消融感知安全信号重分配实现稳健的LLM安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐在面对日益增长的攻击谱时仍然脆弱。越狱攻击通过精心设计的提示绕过安全机制，而神经元级攻击则在部署后直接修剪安全关键神经元。两者都利用了一个共同的弱点：与安全相关的信息集中在稀疏的神经元子集中。我们提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种微调阶段的防御方法，通过在更广泛的神经元集上重新分配安全信号，来同时增强LLMs对这两类攻击的抵御能力。NeuronGuard通过定期更新的每层线性分类器动态识别安全关键神经元，在故意消融神经元时强制拒绝行为，并应用KL散度正则化以保持分布一致性。一种随机梯度投影策略通过解决防御与任务目标之间的冲突，保持下游任务的效用。我们提供了一个正式保证，NeuronGuard严格降低了攻击成功率（ASR）的上限，且在三种LLM、六种最先进的攻击策略和多模态设置下的实验确认了接近零的ASR，同时保持任务准确性，包括对抗白盒自适应对手的情况。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to various safety attacks, specifically jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a limited number of neurons. Previous methods have struggled to provide robust defenses against these attacks, leading to a need for a more effective approach. The proposed method, NeuronGuard, enhances safety alignment by redistributing safety signals across a wider range of neurons, utilizing dynamically refreshed classifiers to identify critical neurons and employing KL-divergence regularization to maintain consistency. This approach not only reduces the attack success rate but also preserves the utility of LLMs for downstream tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that NeuronGuard achieves near-zero attack success rates while maintaining task accuracy across multiple LLMs and attack strategies, effectively supporting its goals of robust safety alignment.

Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次突破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉语提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审核的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种不同的提示条件，变化包括语言、写作风格和权威框架。评估18个前沿LLM时，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%的内容包含严格有害的信息，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时，成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉语内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the critical gap in large language model (LLM) safety evaluation for Bengali, the seventh-most-spoken language, which has been predominantly focused on English. Previous methods have largely overlooked culturally specific harms and have not adequately assessed the safety of LLMs in non-English contexts, leading to unreliable evaluations. The proposed approach, BanglaSafe, introduces a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts that encompass a range of culturally grounded harm categories and prompting conditions, effectively addressing the limitations of existing methods. The study evaluates 18 leading LLMs and reveals that over half of the responses are unsafe, with a notable finding that the writing style in Bengali significantly influences safety outcomes. This research contributes to the understanding of LLM safety in diverse linguistic contexts and highlights the inadequacies of current safety classifiers in evaluating Bengali content, demonstrating the need for culturally aware safety assessments.

Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.

CLEAR: Continuous Latent Adapter Routing for Utility-Preserving LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Chengxiao Wang, Enyi Jiang, Xiaojing Liao, Sanmi Koyejo
First: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Improving the safety of large language models (LLMs) often comes at the expense of utility, as globally applied safety tuning may affect model responses to both harmful and benign inputs. We propose \textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing (CLEAR), a conditional safety adaptation framework that uses a lightweight hidden-state gate to continuously control the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter. CLEAR aims to reduce harmful completions while avoiding unnecessary changes to the frozen backbone that could degrade performance on benign prompts. Experiments on widely used safety and utility benchmarks show that CLEAR improves robustness on HarmBench while reducing the utility degradation observed with globally applied safety tuning such as SFT or standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA). On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reduces HarmBench ASR from 32.3\% to 0.5\%, while retaining most of the base model's utility and achieving up to 7.1 percentage points higher GSM8K accuracy than globally applied SFT or LoRA. These results suggest that CLEAR is a promising mechanism for improving the safety--utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CLEAR：用于保持效用的LLM安全对齐的连续潜在适配器路由
提高大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性往往会以牺牲效用为代价，因为全局应用的安全调优可能会影响模型对有害和良性输入的响应。我们提出了\textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing（CLEAR），这是一种条件安全适应框架，使用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活强度。CLEAR旨在减少有害的完成，同时避免对冻结主干的不必要更改，这可能会降低对良性提示的性能。对广泛使用的安全和效用基准的实验表明，CLEAR在HarmBench上提高了鲁棒性，同时减少了全局应用安全调优（如SFT或标准低秩适配（LoRA））所观察到的效用下降。在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上，CLEAR将HarmBench ASR从32.3\%降低到0.5\%，同时保留了大部分基础模型的效用，并在GSM8K准确率上比全局应用的SFT或LoRA高出多达7.1个百分点。这些结果表明，CLEAR是改善LLM对齐中安全与效用权衡的有希望的机制。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of enhancing the safety of large language models (LLMs) without compromising their utility, as traditional methods like globally applied safety tuning often degrade model performance on benign inputs. The proposed Continuous Latent Adapter Routing (CLEAR) framework introduces a lightweight hidden-state gate that allows for continuous control over the activation of a safety low-rank adapter, thereby minimizing harmful outputs while preserving the model's responses to benign prompts. This approach is well-motivated as it seeks to balance safety and utility effectively. The methodology involves applying CLEAR to various safety and utility benchmarks, demonstrating significant improvements in robustness on HarmBench and a notable reduction in utility degradation compared to standard methods like SFT and LoRA. Specifically, CLEAR achieves a reduction in HarmBench ASR from 32.3% to 0.5% on Llama-3-8B-Instruct, while also increasing GSM8K accuracy by up to 7.1 percentage points, indicating its effectiveness in enhancing the safety-utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
本研究解决了在不影响大型语言模型（LLM）效用的情况下提高其安全性的问题，因为传统的全球安全调优方法往往会降低模型在良性输入上的性能。提出的连续潜在适配器路由（CLEAR）框架引入了一种轻量级的隐藏状态门，可以持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活，从而在减少有害输出的同时保持模型在良性提示上的有效性。这种方法的动机明确，因为它旨在平衡安全性和效用，这是LLM对齐的关键方面。该方法论涉及将CLEAR应用于安全性和效用基准，显示出在HarmBench上的显著稳健性提升，并且与现有方法（如标准微调（SFT）和低秩适配（LoRA））相比，效用降级显著减少。实验表明，CLEAR在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上将HarmBench ASR从32.3%降低到0.5%，同时在GSM8K上实现高达7.1个百分点的准确率提升，表明其在解决安全性与效用权衡方面的有效性。
RouteScan: A Non-Intrusive Approach to Auditing MoE LLMs Safety via Expert Routing Telemetry
Authors: Bo Lv, Zhiheng Xu, KeDong Xiu, Ruyi Ding, Tianhang Zheng, Zhibo Wang, Kui Ren
First: 2026-05-24T02:06:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T12:55:57+00:00
Comments: 11 pages. Revised manuscript with expanded experiments
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures are increasingly adopted for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs), safety auditing becomes necessary to verify whether these models produce or facilitate harmful behaviors during operation. However, existing content-based auditing methods typically require access to user prompts, model internals, or outputs, potentially exposing sensitive user information and creating a tension between LLM safety and user privacy. On the other hand, we observe that, in MoE models, different inputs induce different sparse expert-routing patterns, which produce measurable footprints in low-level GPU execution telemetry. We refer to these hardware-observable signals induced by expert-routing decisions as expert routing telemetry; they are derived from GPU execution rather than from router logits or token-level routing assignments. Inspired by this observation, we propose RouteScan, a non-intrusive auditing framework for detecting harmful behaviors through such routing-induced GPU telemetry. Specifically, RouteScan utilizes the number of active GPU threads allocated to expert modules during the prefilling phase as a discriminative micro-architectural fingerprint, and builds a lightweight detection pipeline that isolates cross-domain invariant risk indicators for the precise identification of malicious prompts. Comprehensive evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs with distinct routing designs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves strong generalization, with an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains. Moreover, privacy stress tests show that, although aggregated execution telemetry retains input-related attribute information, full prompts and exact sensitive fields cannot be reliably recovered under the evaluated attacks.

随着混合专家（MoE）架构在大规模语言模型（LLM）中的日益采用，安全审计变得必要，以验证这些模型在操作过程中是否产生或促进有害行为。然而，现有的基于内容的审计方法通常需要访问用户提示、模型内部或输出，可能会暴露敏感用户信息，并在LLM安全性与用户隐私之间产生紧张关系。另一方面，我们观察到，在MoE模型中，不同的输入会引发不同的稀疏专家路由模式，这在低级GPU执行遥测中产生可测量的足迹。我们将这些由专家路由决策引起的硬件可观察信号称为专家路由遥测；它们源自GPU执行，而不是来自路由器logits或令牌级路由分配。受到这一观察的启发，我们提出了RouteScan，一种通过这种路由引起的GPU遥测检测有害行为的非侵入式审计框架。具体而言，RouteScan利用在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活跃GPU线程数量作为区分性微架构指纹，并构建一个轻量级检测管道，隔离跨域不变风险指标，以精确识别恶意提示。对四个具有不同路由设计的开源MoE LLM的全面评估表明，RouteScan实现了强大的泛化能力，在未见的有害领域中AUROC超过0.91。此外，隐私压力测试表明，尽管聚合的执行遥测保留了与输入相关的属性信息，但在评估的攻击下，无法可靠地恢复完整提示和确切的敏感字段。

Certified Multi-Turn Robustness for LLM Safety via Compositional Bounds and Safety Persistence
Authors: Yang Liu, Bin Chong, Wenkai Yang, Shuai Zhang, Yancheng Chen, Feiyu Han, GuoZhen, Cheng Zhang, Huaibing Xie, Changze Lv, Shihan Dou, Pluto Zhou
First: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that progressively manipulate conversation context. Existing certified robustness methods are limited to single-turn inputs; naive multi-turn composition yields bounds that degrade exponentially in the number of turns. We introduce Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), a framework that models conversational safety via State-Adversarial MDPs and defines $k$-turn certified robustness as the worst-case safety probability across $k$ adversarial turns. MTCR comprises: (i) compositional certification via embedding-space mode decomposition, yielding tighter certified lower bounds than naive multiplication; (ii) $(α,β)$-safety persistence, improving the degradation rate from $\underline{p}^{k}$ to $β^k$ (with $β> \underline{p}$) and yielding interpretable horizon estimates; (iii) matching information-theoretic upper bounds establishing tightness; and (iv) a unified algorithm combining these results. Experiments on six LLMs under $ε$-bounded and Crescendo-style attacks confirm that empirical safety consistently exceeds the certified bounds.
中文标题/摘要

Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:17:49+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for the utility of an expert call, as it ignores whether the expert would actually improve the prediction. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on either computation cost or safety performance while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that predicts the benefit of replacing the probe score with the expert score after an expert call. CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees while optimising the complementary objective. Evaluated on four safety datasets using learned-attention probes, CTD improves over uncertainty-based delegation over much of the budget range, avoids unnecessary expert calls, and extracts positive-value subsets even from groups where the expert score is worse than the probe score on average. Ablations across four probe architectures show that these findings are not specific to any particular architecture.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委派：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监测
大规模监测LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委派，但不确定性并不是专家调用效用的良好代理，因为它忽略了专家是否真的会改善预测。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委派（CTD），一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委派价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上运行，预测在专家调用后用专家分数替代探测器分数的好处。CTD使用保留数据和多重假设检验对DV信号进行阈值校准，提供有限样本保证，同时优化互补目标。在四个安全数据集上使用学习注意力探测器进行评估，CTD在大部分预算范围内优于基于不确定性的委派，避免了不必要的专家调用，并从专家分数平均低于探测器分数的组中提取出正值子集。对四种探测器架构的消融实验表明，这些发现并不特定于任何特定架构。

The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where there is a need to balance cost and accuracy in decision-making. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty to delegate tasks, which proved inadequate as it did not account for whether expert intervention would actually enhance predictions. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) method introduces a model-cascade approach that utilizes a novel delegation value (DV) probe to assess the potential benefit of expert calls, thus providing probabilistic guarantees on computation cost or safety performance. 本研究解决了大规模监测大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性的问题，重点关注在安全评估中平衡成本和准确性的需求。以往的方法依赖于探测器的不确定性来将案例委托给专家模型，但这种方法往往错误地表示专家评估的实际效用，导致资源使用效率低下。提出的Calibrate-Then-Delegate（CTD）方法引入了一种新颖的委托值（DV）探测器，能够预测专家干预的收益，从而实现更为明智的决策，同时提供计算成本和安全性能的保证。CTD在四个安全数据集上进行了评估，结果显示其在传统的不确定性基础委托上表现出更好的性能，有效减少了不必要的专家调用并优化了资源分配，从而支持其在LLMs安全监测中的目标。
aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy
Authors: Fatih Deniz, Yazan Boshmaf, Dorde Popovic, Issa Khalil
First: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The critical failure modes in deployed large language models (LLMs) are cross-dimensional: a model can score 99.3 in safety alignment while refusing one in three benign queries, or improve across every capability metric while losing 21 points in privacy. Existing evaluation frameworks that assess safety, security, and privacy independently cannot detect these patterns. We introduce aiXamine, a unified black-box platform that evaluates LLM trustworthiness across safety, security, and privacy as interdependent properties. aiXamine orchestrates 46 tests across nine services through an automated red-teaming pipeline, producing hierarchical risk profiles, from prompt-level diagnostics to cross-service trade-off analytics, that enable reproducible comparison of proprietary and open-weight systems under identical conditions. Applying aiXamine to over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, we conduct the largest joint safety, security, and privacy study to date and uncover three cross-dimensional phenomena invisible to single-axis evaluation. First, safety enforcement incurs a quantifiable safety tax: stronger alignment systematically increases over-refusal, forcing providers to choose between protection and utility. Second, privacy is near-orthogonal to other trustworthiness dimensions and not captured by standard alignment. Third, we identify and formally characterize distillation-induced robustness collapse: off-policy distillation without on-policy correction causes entropy collapse, catastrophically destroying robustness (56.9$\to$2.6) on the same base architecture. These findings, compounded by diminishing returns from scale and category-dependent safety behaviors, demonstrate that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional: progress along one axis does not guarantee, and can actively undermine, progress along others, yet current alignment methods treat it as a single objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：aiXamine：统一黑箱评估大型语言模型安全性、保密性和隐私中的跨维权衡
已部署的大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失效模式是跨维的：一个模型在安全对齐上得分99.3，但拒绝三分之一的良性查询，或者在每个能力指标上都有所提升，但隐私损失21分。现有的评估框架独立评估安全性、保密性和隐私，无法检测这些模式。我们引入了aiXamine，一个统一的黑箱平台，评估LLM在安全性、保密性和隐私方面的可信度，作为相互依赖的属性。aiXamine通过自动化红队管道协调46个测试，涵盖九项服务，生成从提示级诊断到跨服务权衡分析的分层风险概况，使在相同条件下对专有和开放权重系统的可重复比较成为可能。通过超过5000次测试运行将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM，我们进行了迄今为止最大的联合安全性、保密性和隐私研究，揭示了三种在单轴评估中不可见的跨维现象。首先，安全执行会产生可量化的安全税：更强的对齐系统性地增加过度拒绝，迫使提供者在保护和效用之间做出选择。其次，隐私与其他可信度维度几乎正交，标准对齐无法捕捉。第三，我们识别并正式表征了蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃：没有政策校正的离政策蒸馏导致熵崩溃，灾难性地破坏鲁棒性（56.9$\to$2.6）在相同的基础架构上。这些发现，加上规模的递减收益和类别依赖的安全行为，表明可信度本质上是多维的：在一个轴上的进展并不保证，并且可能会积极破坏其他轴上的进展，而当前的对齐方法将其视为单一目标。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical failure modes in large language models (LLMs), which often exhibit interdependent issues in safety, security, and privacy that existing evaluation frameworks fail to capture due to their independent assessments. Traditional methods do not account for the complex trade-offs between these dimensions, leading to incomplete evaluations. The proposed approach, aiXamine, offers a unified black-box evaluation platform that assesses LLM trustworthiness across these interdependent properties through an automated red-teaming pipeline, conducting 46 tests across nine services. This methodology allows for the generation of hierarchical risk profiles and facilitates reproducible comparisons of different systems. The application of aiXamine to over 120 LLMs revealed significant findings, including the identification of a safety tax, the near-orthogonality of privacy to other dimensions, and the phenomenon of distillation-induced robustness collapse, demonstrating that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional and that improvements in one area can negatively impact others.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失效模式，这些模式在安全性、安全性和隐私之间存在跨维度权衡，而现有的评估框架由于独立评估而无法检测到这些问题。以往的方法未能充分捕捉这些属性之间的相互依赖性，导致评估不完整。提出的方法aiXamine是一个统一的黑箱评估平台，自动评估LLM在这些维度上的可信度，进行46项测试，涵盖九个服务，并生成全面的风险概况。这种方法允许在相同条件下对各种LLM进行可重复的比较。将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM的研究揭示了重要发现，包括可量化的安全税、隐私与其他维度的近正交性，以及蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃的识别，表明可信度是多维的，并且在一个领域的改进可能会对其他领域产生负面影响。
Enforcing LLM Safety through DMD-based Classification of Prompt-Response Embedding Dynamics
Authors: Mohamed Akrout, Olivera Kotevska, Dan Wilson
First: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes applications, yet their tendency to generate toxic, harmful, or policy-violating content poses significant risks. Detecting these unsafe outputs efficiently in a black-box manner remains an open challenge. In this paper, we extend a recently proposed dynamical systems framework designed for hallucination detection to LLM safety classification. By projecting both prompts and responses into high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe regimes, we classify new outputs using a new differential residual score that compares prediction errors of the safe and unsafe regimes. A key contribution is the incorporation of the prompt and response embedding dynamics, yielding fitted Koopman operators that capture crucial interaction patterns. We evaluate our black-box method across three safety benchmarks using three embedding models. Our results show that incorporating prompt embeddings yields consistent improvements, particularly for interaction-dependent violations when paired with causal decoders (e.g., in Llama-3), while response-only violations benefit more from dense semantic embedding representations. These findings opens the door for using dynamical systems to analyze AI systems rather than the dominant paradigm of using AI to model dynamical systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过基于DMD的提示-响应嵌入动态分类来强化LLM安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险场景，但它们生成有毒、有害或违反政策内容的倾向带来了重大风险。以黑箱方式高效检测这些不安全输出仍然是一个开放挑战。本文扩展了最近提出的用于幻觉检测的动态系统框架，以进行LLM安全分类。通过将提示和响应投影到高维嵌入空间，并为安全和不安全状态拟合独立的基于Koopman的预测模型，我们使用新的微分残差分数对新输出进行分类，该分数比较安全和不安全状态的预测误差。一个关键贡献是结合了提示和响应的嵌入动态，得到了捕捉关键交互模式的拟合Koopman算子。我们在三个安全基准上使用三种嵌入模型评估了我们的黑箱方法。结果表明，结合提示嵌入可以带来一致的改进，特别是在与因果解码器（例如Llama-3）配对时，针对交互依赖的违规行为，而仅响应的违规行为则更受益于密集语义嵌入表示。这些发现为使用动态系统分析AI系统打开了大门，而不是使用AI建模动态系统的主流范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical issue of safety in Large Language Models (LLMs), which are prone to generating harmful content, posing risks in high-stakes applications. Previous methods for detecting unsafe outputs have struggled with efficiency and effectiveness in a black-box context. This paper proposes a novel approach that extends a dynamical systems framework for hallucination detection to classify LLM safety by utilizing high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe outputs. The methodology involves analyzing prompt and response embedding dynamics to derive a differential residual score that effectively distinguishes between safe and unsafe outputs. The proposed method demonstrates significant performance improvements across three safety benchmarks, particularly in scenarios involving interaction-dependent violations, thereby supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety classification.

Fair ASR: Re-Evaluating Black-Box Jailbreaks under Shared Target-Call Budgets
Authors: Zhida He, Xiaoyu Wen, Han Qi, Ziyuan Zhou, Peng Yu, Jiajia Li, Chaochao Lu, Qiaosheng Zhang
First: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00
Comments: 29 pages, 8 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/xsddys/Fair-ASR
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Reliable jailbreak evaluation is essential for assessing LLM safety, but most existing studies rely solely on attack success rate (ASR) without accounting for its dependence on attack budgets, resulting in unfair comparisons across methods. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce heterogeneous resources into FLOPs, which is difficult to estimate for black-box models and fails to capture resource-specific constraints. To provide a comparable evaluation basis, we introduce Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol for black-box jailbreak attacks under shared target-call budgets B, using target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. We re-evaluate 11 representative attacks under the Fair-ASR protocol and find that attack rankings change substantially across target-call budgets, simple stochastic perturbations and hand-crafted templates remain highly competitive under equal target access, and no evaluated LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. Motivated by this efficiency gap, we introduce ReCode, a compositional budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with two effective low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR. Under a budget of 20 target calls, ReCode achieves 85% ASR on GPT-5 while requiring only 7.19 attacker calls per request on average, showing strong efficiency in both target and attacker calls.
中文标题/摘要
标题：公平ASR：在共享目标调用预算下重新评估黑箱越狱
可靠的越狱评估对于评估大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性至关重要，但现有大多数研究仅依赖攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑其对攻击预算的依赖，导致方法之间的不公平比较。现有的计算感知评估将异构资源简化为FLOPs，这对于黑箱模型难以估计，并未捕捉资源特定的约束。为了提供可比较的评估基础，我们引入了公平ASR，这是一种在共享目标调用预算B下评估黑箱越狱攻击的评估协议，使用目标调用作为直接可观察和方法无关的比较轴，同时单独跟踪攻击者调用以进行效率分析。我们在公平ASR协议下重新评估了11个代表性攻击，发现攻击排名在目标调用预算之间发生了显著变化，简单的随机扰动和手工模板在平等目标访问下仍然具有高度竞争力，并且没有评估的LLM驱动方法在目标和攻击者调用中都有效率。受到这一效率差距的启发，我们引入了ReCode，这是一种组合预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和公平ASR识别的两种有效低成本原语。在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，同时平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，显示出在目标和攻击者调用中的强效率。

Making Your LLMs More Objective: Stabilizing LLM Safety Behavior Across Traits with Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning
Authors: Lang Cao
First: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligned large language models (LLMs) are expected to exhibit safety behavior based on the content of the user request: they should refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones. However, we show that the same request can elicit substantially different safety decisions under different traits assigned in the system prompt, a failure mode we call trait-induced safety variation. To measure this failure, we introduce refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation measures dataset-level deviation from the no-trait baseline, while Trait-Induced Flip Rate measures whether the same request receives different safety decisions across traits. We then provide a representation-level analysis of the mechanism behind trait-induced safety shifts and find that traits perturb the model's safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. To achieve trait-invariant safety, where safety behavior remains stable across traits, we introduce Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), a simple yet effective self-distillation framework that aligns an LLM's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior. Guided by our analysis, we further propose Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), an instantiation of TIST, which enforces invariance only within the identified trait subspace. Experiments show that TraSN improves trait-invariant safety and strengthens harmful-request safety while preserving general capability. Our results highlight traits as an important factor in LLM safety and robust model behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使您的大型语言模型更客观：通过特征不变安全调优稳定大型语言模型的安全行为
对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）预计会根据用户请求的内容表现出安全行为：它们应该拒绝不安全的请求并遵守安全的请求。然而，我们展示了同一请求在系统提示中分配的不同特征下可能引发显著不同的安全决策，这种失败模式我们称之为特征引起的安全变异。为了衡量这种失败，我们引入了基于拒绝的指标：特征引起的偏差衡量数据集级别的偏差，而特征引起的翻转率衡量同一请求在不同特征下是否收到不同的安全决策。然后，我们提供了特征引起的安全变化机制的表示级分析，发现特征在低维子空间内扰动模型的安全表示。为了实现特征不变安全，即安全行为在不同特征间保持稳定，我们引入了特征不变安全调优（TIST），这是一种简单而有效的自蒸馏框架，将LLM的特征条件行为与其无特征行为对齐。在我们的分析指导下，我们进一步提出了特征子空间中和（TraSN），这是TIST的一种实例，它仅在识别的特征子空间内强制不变性。实验表明，TraSN提高了特征不变安全性，并增强了有害请求的安全性，同时保持了整体能力。我们的结果强调特征是LLM安全性和稳健模型行为的重要因素。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the issue of safety behavior in aligned large language models (LLMs), which can vary significantly based on different traits assigned in system prompts, leading to inconsistent safety decisions—a phenomenon termed trait-induced safety variation. Previous methods did not adequately account for this variability, resulting in unreliable safety responses. The proposed approach, Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), aims to stabilize safety behavior across traits by aligning the model's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior, utilizing a self-distillation framework. The paper contributes by introducing refusal-based metrics to measure safety deviations and presenting Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN) to enforce invariance within a specific trait subspace. Experimental results demonstrate that TraSN enhances trait-invariant safety and improves responses to harmful requests while maintaining overall model capabilities, thus supporting the goal of consistent safety behavior across varying traits.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）在系统提示中不同特征分配下安全行为显著不同的问题，这导致安全决策不一致。以往的方法未能充分考虑这种特征引起的安全变化，导致安全响应不可靠。提出的方法特征不变安全调优（TIST）旨在通过将模型的特征条件行为与无特征行为对齐，利用自蒸馏框架来稳定不同特征下的安全行为。本文的贡献在于引入拒绝基础指标来测量安全偏差，并提出特征子空间中立化（TraSN）以在特定特征子空间内强制不变性。实验结果表明，TraSN增强了特征不变安全性，并改善了对有害请求的响应，同时保持了整体模型能力，从而支持了LLMs中一致安全行为的目标。
ProbGuard: Calibrated Safety Risk Estimation from LLM Output Distributions
Authors: Xinzhe Huang, Biwu Yao, Kedong Xiu, Mengnan Zhao, Di Wang, Puning Zhao, Tianhang Zheng
First: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Recent research on Large Language Model (LLM) safety has widely adopted guardrails to identify unsafe LLM outputs. Existing guardrails typically formulate safety assessment as a deterministic classification task, mapping a discrete token sequence to a discrete safety label. However, this paradigm has two limitations: First, safety assessment is inherently an uncertain problem, particularly during the early generation state. Second, relying solely on discrete token sequences discards the rich probabilistic information embedded in the LLM output distribution. To address these limitations, we propose the first completely probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail \textsc{ProbGuard} to leverage the LLM early output distributional signals for estimating and calibrating the safety probability, thereby enabling early stopping of unsafe ongoing outputs. Specifically, given an LLM's generated prefix distribution, we formulate the safety risk as the unsafe probability of its continued generation dynamics and estimate this risk by Monte-Carlo sampling. Through post-training on the distributional signals and calibrated safety risk, \textsc{ProbGuard} achieves the best calibration performance across all nine model--dataset combination settings, reducing the average Brier score and ECE by 79.6\% and 71.9\%, respectively, over the best baseline. \textsc{ProbGuard} further limits the attack success rate to at most 1\% across six representative jailbreak attacks after observing the LLM early output distributions from only the first ten decoding steps.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ProbGuard：基于LLM输出分布的校准安全风险估计
近期对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的研究广泛采用了保护措施来识别不安全的LLM输出。现有的保护措施通常将安全评估视为一个确定性分类任务，将离散的标记序列映射到离散的安全标签。然而，这一范式存在两个局限性：首先，安全评估本质上是一个不确定性问题，特别是在早期生成阶段。其次，仅依赖离散标记序列会丢弃LLM输出分布中嵌入的丰富概率信息。为了解决这些局限性，我们提出了第一个完全概率架构无关的保护措施\textsc{ProbGuard}，利用LLM早期输出的分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而实现对不安全持续输出的早期停止。具体而言，给定LLM生成的前缀分布，我们将安全风险表述为其持续生成动态的不安全概率，并通过蒙特卡洛采样来估计这一风险。通过对分布信号和校准安全风险的后训练，\textsc{ProbGuard}在所有九个模型-数据集组合设置中实现了最佳的校准性能，平均Brier分数和ECE分别减少了79.6\%和71.9\%，超越了最佳基线。\textsc{ProbGuard}进一步将六个代表性越狱攻击中的攻击成功率限制在最多1\%以内，仅在观察到LLM前十个解码步骤的早期输出分布后。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the limitations of existing safety assessment methods for Large Language Models (LLMs), which typically treat safety as a deterministic classification task, neglecting the inherent uncertainty and rich probabilistic information in LLM outputs. The proposed approach, ProbGuard, introduces a probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail that utilizes early output distribution signals to estimate and calibrate safety probabilities, allowing for early termination of unsafe outputs. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative use of Monte-Carlo sampling to assess safety risk based on LLM's generated prefix distribution, resulting in significant improvements in calibration performance, with reductions of 79.6% in average Brier score and 71.9% in expected calibration error across various model-dataset combinations. Additionally, ProbGuard effectively limits attack success rates to 1% in six jailbreak scenarios after analyzing early output distributions from just the first ten decoding steps.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）安全评估方法的局限性，这些方法通常将安全性视为确定性分类任务，忽视了LLM输出中固有的不确定性和丰富的概率信息。所提出的方法ProbGuard通过利用完全概率的架构无关框架，利用早期输出分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而允许在不安全输出的情况下提前终止。这种方法的动机明确，因为它直接应对了安全评估中的不确定性，并增强了对概率数据的使用。本文的贡献在于通过对LLM生成的前缀分布进行蒙特卡洛采样，提出了安全风险的新公式，在各种模型-数据集组合中实现了校准性能的显著提升，平均Brier分数降低了79.6%，预期校准误差降低了71.9%。此外，ProbGuard在仅分析前十个解码步骤的早期输出分布后，有效地将多个越狱场景中的攻击成功率限制在1%。
Agent Safety Should Be a Runtime Contract
Authors: Albus W. Ng, Yi Han, Jusheng Zhang, Wenhao Wang
First: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The dominant paradigm treats AI safety as a property to be instilled during model training via RLHF, DPO, or Constitutional AI. We argue this is structurally insufficient for autonomous agents that execute code, mutate files, send messages, and modify databases. Agent safety should be a runtime contract enforced by the harness, and the contract has two complementary faces. The preventive face blocks dangerous actions before they happen via sandboxes, permission gates, output filters, and trajectory monitors. The evidential face requires verifiable proof that good actions actually happened, gating task submission on hard evidence such as test runs, log captures, file diffs, and citation grounding. We ground the position in four lines of public evidence, with row-level protocols and data released in the supplementary JSON files: a survey of 52 documented AI-agent and LLM safety incidents, a false-completion audit with 31 non-contested core cases plus one disputed illustrative case, a trajectory-schema audit of 12 public agent systems and harnesses, and a title-level audit of all 28,560 papers accepted at NeurIPS, ICML, and ICLR 2023-2025 showing a pooled 8-12x imbalance between training-time and deployment-time publication. Two prior communities that needed to enforce safety, computer security and the experimental sciences, converged on runtime contracts with both preventive and evidential elements; agentic AI is now under the same pressure. We formalize an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, state a compositional gating proposition based on standard monitor composition, and outline a research agenda. The right unit of safety in agentic AI is the trajectory-with-checkable-evidence, not the model.
中文标题/摘要
标题：代理安全应为运行时契约
主流范式将AI安全视为在模型训练期间通过RLHF、DPO或宪法AI灌输的属性。我们认为这对于执行代码、修改文件、发送消息和修改数据库的自主代理来说结构上是不够的。代理安全应由安全框架强制执行的运行时契约，该契约有两个互补的方面。预防性方面通过沙箱、权限门、输出过滤器和轨迹监控在危险行为发生之前阻止它们。证据性方面要求可验证的证据证明良好行为确实发生，任务提交需基于硬证据，如测试运行、日志捕获、文件差异和引用基础。我们在四条公共证据中支持这一立场，附加JSON文件中发布了行级协议和数据：对52起记录的AI代理和LLM安全事件的调查、31个无争议核心案例加一个有争议示例案例的虚假完成审计、12个公共代理系统和安全框架的轨迹模式审计，以及对2023-2025年NeurIPS、ICML和ICLR接受的28,560篇论文的标题级审计，显示训练时间和部署时间出版之间的8-12倍不平衡。两个需要强制执行安全的先前社区，计算机安全和实验科学，已在运行时契约上达成共识，包含预防性和证据性元素；代理AI现在也面临同样的压力。我们形式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，基于标准监控组合提出了一个组合门控命题，并概述了研究议程。代理AI中的安全正确单位是带可检查证据的轨迹，而不是模型。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the inadequacies of current AI safety paradigms, which primarily focus on instilling safety during model training through methods like RLHF and DPO. The authors argue that these approaches are insufficient for autonomous agents that perform complex actions, necessitating a shift towards treating agent safety as a runtime contract. This proposed method introduces a dual-faceted contract: a preventive face that blocks harmful actions and an evidential face that requires verifiable proof of safe actions. The paper contributes by presenting an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, drawing parallels with established practices in computer security and experimental sciences. The methodology emphasizes the importance of trajectory-based safety with checkable evidence, and the authors outline a research agenda to further explore this framework, aiming to enhance safety measures in AI deployment.
本文探讨了当前AI安全范式的不足之处，主要集中在通过RLHF、DPO或宪法AI等方法在模型训练期间灌输安全性。这些方法对于执行各种操作的自主代理而言是不够的，因此需要转向运行时合同，通过预防和证据机制来强制执行安全性。所提出的方法包括使用沙箱、权限网关、输出过滤器和轨迹监控来防止危险行为，同时要求通过日志和测试运行提供安全行为的可验证证据。本文的贡献在于形式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，并强调了确保代理AI安全所需的全面研究议程。研究结果通过广泛的审计和调查得以支持，显示出训练和部署阶段之间安全实践的显著不平衡，从而进一步强化了在AI安全中实施运行时合同的必要性。
HoloAegis: Frozen Representation, Topological Inference: Minimally Parametric Safety Manifolds for Zero-Shot LLM Guardrails
Authors: Tak Ho Alex Li, Kaijie Liu, Lik-Hang Lee, Kin Chung Ho, Ping Shum, Michael K. Ng
First: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00
Comments: Preprint, August 2026. 10 tables, 2 figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety guardrails face a fundamental tension: fine-tuning distorts pre-trained representations while generative judges incur prohibitive inference costs. We challenge the prevailing paradigm by asking: can safety be achieved through pure geometric reasoning over frozen semantic representations? We present HoloAegis, a minimally parametric topological inference framework that decouples representation from reasoning. We term our approach minimally parametric because the only free parameters are the anchor count K and the temperature tau, both fixed after construction and requiring no gradient-based training. An un-fine-tuned encoder maps text to a unit sphere, after which all decisions are purely geometric. We formalize safety evaluation as a Gibbs-Boltzmann Free Energy computation over a pre-computed System Topology Anchor Bank, and we introduce Dual Time-Scale Exponential Moving Averages to detect progressive multi-turn semantic drift. Our key theoretical insight is a Topological Boundary Stability Conjecture: we provide theoretical motivation and strong empirical evidence that sparse anchor centroids stabilize the decision boundary against high-frequency lexical perturbations far better than full vector space methods. Evaluated across 8 benchmarks, HoloAegis achieves state-of-the-art accuracy (1.0000 AUC on AuthenHallu, 0.9802 on HarmBench) with sub-millisecond latency, zero cold-start data, and cross-lingual transfer (0.9758 AUC on Chinese CHIFRAUD).
中文标题/摘要
标题：HoloAegis：冻结表示、拓扑推理：零样本LLM安全防护的最小参数安全流形
当前LLM安全防护面临根本性矛盾：微调扭曲了预训练表示，而生成性判断则产生了高昂的推理成本。我们通过提出一个问题来挑战现有范式：是否可以通过对冻结语义表示的纯几何推理来实现安全？我们提出HoloAegis，一个最小参数的拓扑推理框架，将表示与推理解耦。我们称这种方法为最小参数，因为唯一的自由参数是锚点数量K和温度tau，构建后均为固定值且无需基于梯度的训练。未微调的编码器将文本映射到单位球体，之后所有决策均为纯几何。我们将安全评估形式化为对预计算系统拓扑锚库的Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能计算，并引入双时间尺度指数移动平均来检测渐进的多轮语义漂移。我们的关键理论见解是拓扑边界稳定性猜想：我们提供了理论动机和强有力的实证证据，表明稀疏锚心在抵御高频词汇扰动方面比全向量空间方法更能稳定决策边界。在8个基准测试中评估，HoloAegis实现了最先进的准确率（AuthenHallu上1.0000 AUC，HarmBench上0.9802），具有亚毫秒延迟、零冷启动数据和跨语言迁移（在中文CHIFRAUD上0.9758 AUC）。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of current large language model (LLM) safety guardrails, which often distort pre-trained representations through fine-tuning or incur high inference costs with generative judges. The proposed method, HoloAegis, introduces a minimally parametric topological inference framework that utilizes frozen semantic representations, allowing for geometric reasoning without the need for gradient-based training. This approach is motivated by the need for stability in decision boundaries against lexical perturbations, which is achieved through a Topological Boundary Stability Conjecture and the use of sparse anchor centroids. HoloAegis demonstrates significant contributions by achieving state-of-the-art accuracy across eight benchmarks, including a perfect AUC score on AuthenHallu and high performance on cross-lingual tasks, all while maintaining low latency and requiring no cold-start data.
本研究解决了当前大型语言模型（LLM）安全护栏的局限性，这些护栏通常通过微调扭曲预训练表示，或通过生成判断器产生高推理成本。提出的方法HoloAegis引入了一种最小参数拓扑推理框架，利用冻结的语义表示，允许在不进行基于梯度的训练的情况下进行几何推理。这种方法的动机在于需要对抗词汇扰动的决策边界稳定性，通过拓扑边界稳定性猜想和稀疏锚心的使用来实现。HoloAegis在八个基准测试中表现出显著的贡献，包括在AuthenHallu上达到1.0000的AUC和在HarmBench上达到0.9802，同时保持低延迟并支持跨语言迁移，从而有效地实现其安全目标。
Yesterday's Shield, Today's Spear: A Self-Evolving Safety Guardrail in Production
Authors: Cong Ming, Jingyi Chen, Bin Liu, Qi Chu, Tao Gong, Nenghai Yu, Yingfei Xiang
First: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Deployed LLM safety guardrails are predominantly static: trained once and frozen at release, while new jailbreak techniques and previously un-addressed harmful categories emerge within days, leaving the defense perpetually a step behind. We present SESG (Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails), a multi-agent system running in production. SESG monitors the live traffic behind a deployed guardrail and surfaces two classes of failure: jailbreaks novel in form and harmful categories novel in content. Once a failure is confirmed, a generation agent synthesizes paired training data targeted at it; a validation agent rebalances the batch toward the direction in which the deployed model errs, so that the model's own mistakes steer its training set; and a routing agent matches the training action to the diagnosed gap and returns the next version to production. Over six rounds of live evolution (V0 to V6), a 1.7B guardrail adapts to a new threat in 16-24 hours, with about 2 hours of human effort, versus the 40-90 hours of the manual process it replaces. On six emerging threats, it outperforms static guardrails from 0.6B to 9B and an adaptive baseline while preserving its general screening competence. Since April 2026, SESG has been the primary update pipeline of Sangfor's guardrail, autonomously closing 14 of 15 new threat scenarios in two months. We release 9 test sets for the 6 new threats at https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG. Warning: This paper contains examples that may be harmful or offensive.
中文标题/摘要
标题：昨日的护盾，今日的矛：生产中的自我进化安全护栏
部署的LLM安全护栏主要是静态的：训练一次后在发布时冻结，而新的越狱技术和之前未解决的有害类别在几天内出现，使防御始终滞后。我们提出了SESG（自我进化安全护栏），这是一个在生产中运行的多智能体系统。SESG监控部署护栏后面的实时流量，并揭示两类失败：形式新颖的越狱和内容新颖的有害类别。一旦确认失败，生成代理合成针对该失败的配对训练数据；验证代理将批次重新平衡到部署模型出错的方向，以便模型自身的错误引导其训练集；路由代理将训练动作与诊断出的差距匹配，并将下一个版本返回生产。在六轮实时进化（V0到V6）中，一个1.7B的护栏在16-24小时内适应新威胁，约需2小时的人力，而替代的手动过程需40-90小时。在六个新兴威胁中，它的表现优于从0.6B到9B的静态护栏和自适应基线，同时保持其一般筛选能力。自2026年4月以来，SESG已成为Sangfor护栏的主要更新管道，在两个月内自主关闭15个新威胁场景中的14个。我们在https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG发布了6个新威胁的9个测试集。警告：本文包含可能有害或冒犯的示例。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of static safety guardrails in large language models (LLMs), which become outdated quickly due to emerging jailbreak techniques and harmful content categories. Previous methods relied on manual updates, which were time-consuming and inefficient, taking 40-90 hours to adapt to new threats. The proposed Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails (SESG) system introduces a multi-agent approach that autonomously monitors live traffic, identifies failures, and generates targeted training data to adapt the guardrail within 16-24 hours with only 2 hours of human intervention. This method significantly improves response time and effectiveness, achieving superior performance against six emerging threats compared to both static guardrails and an adaptive baseline, while maintaining general screening capabilities. Since its deployment in April 2026, SESG has effectively addressed 14 out of 15 new threat scenarios within two months, demonstrating its practical applicability and efficiency in enhancing LLM safety.

Automating Deception: Scalable Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Adarsh Kumarappan, Ananya Mujoo
First: 2025-11-24T03:15:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-08T15:49:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn conversational attacks, which leverage psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD), where a small initial request paves the way for a more significant one, to bypass safety alignments, pose a persistent threat to Large Language Models (LLMs). Progress in defending against these attacks is hindered by a reliance on manual, hard-to-scale dataset creation. This paper introduces a novel, automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-grounded multi-turn jailbreak datasets. We systematically operationalize FITD techniques into reproducible templates, creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios across illegal activities and offensive content. We evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn (with history) and single-turn (without history) conditions. Our results reveal stark differences in contextual robustness: models in the GPT family demonstrate a significant vulnerability to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates (ASR) increasing by as much as 32 percentage points. In contrast, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash exhibits exceptional resilience, proving nearly immune to these attacks, while Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku shows strong but imperfect resistance. These findings highlight a critical divergence in how current safety architectures handle conversational context and underscore the need for defenses that can resist narrative-based manipulation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自动化欺骗：可扩展的多轮LLM越狱
多轮对话攻击利用心理学原理，如“脚踏实地”（FITD），通过小的初始请求为更大的请求铺平道路，从而绕过安全对齐，对大型语言模型（LLM）构成持续威胁。防御这些攻击的进展受到依赖于手动、难以扩展的数据集创建的限制。本文介绍了一种新颖的自动化管道，用于生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集。我们系统地将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，创建了一个包含1500个场景的基准，涵盖非法活动和攻击性内容。我们在多轮（带历史）和单轮（无历史）条件下评估了来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型。我们的结果揭示了上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异：GPT家族的模型对对话历史表现出显著脆弱性，攻击成功率（ASR）增加了多达32个百分点。相比之下，谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出卓越的韧性，几乎对这些攻击免疫，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则显示出强但不完美的抵抗力。这些发现突显了当前安全架构在处理对话上下文方面的关键分歧，并强调了需要能够抵御叙事操控的防御措施。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the persistent threat posed by multi-turn conversational attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs), which exploit psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD) to bypass safety measures. Previous methods relied on manual dataset creation, which is difficult to scale and limits the effectiveness of defenses against such attacks. The proposed approach automates the generation of large-scale, psychologically-informed multi-turn jailbreak datasets, operationalizing FITD techniques into reproducible templates and creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios. The methodology involves evaluating seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn and single-turn conditions, revealing significant differences in contextual robustness. The findings indicate that while GPT models are notably vulnerable to conversational history, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash shows remarkable resilience, and Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku demonstrates strong but imperfect resistance, emphasizing the need for improved defenses against narrative-based manipulation.
